# EG-DECISION-ENGINE requirements

| ID | Requirement | Verification |
|---|---|---|
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R001` | **Typed decision ladder with explicit resolution kinds (rollup).** Every decision answer exposes a resolution_kind of constraint, entailment, optimization, statistical, or abstention, selected by applying policy and hard constraints first, then ontology entailment, then bounded exact solving, and falling back to fitted statistics only to rank remaining legal options or abstain. Split (2026-10-09): `.1` the three resolution kinds the production chokepoint actually emits, `.2` the missing Constraint/Entailment production paths. Child `.100` adds a GraphOS pre-surface physical/safety bound decision contract. It does not replace GraphOS's required served surfacing gate. | Unit and integration tests assert the resolution_kind returned for representative constraint, entailment, optimization, statistical, and abstention cases. `.100` requires served bound checks and an explicit GraphOS pre-surface consumer receipt. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R001.1` | **`ResolutionKind` is typed with all five variants; the production chokepoint emits Optimization, Statistical, and Abstention.** `ResolutionKind` (crates/eg-types) defines Constraint, Entailment, Optimization, Statistical, and Abstention. The live `Decide`/assemble chokepoint (`crates/eg-compute/src/assemble/conclude.rs`) constructs `ResolutionKind::Optimization` and `ResolutionKind::Abstention`; `stat_decide` constructs `ResolutionKind::Statistical` and `::Abstention`. | `crates/eg-compute/src/assemble/tests/routing.rs` and `crates/eg-types/src/test_support/contract_wave/decision.rs` assert resolution_kind for Optimization/Statistical/Abstention cases. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R001.2` | **Remaining scope: production Constraint and Entailment resolution paths.** `conclude.rs` and `stat_decide` never construct `ResolutionKind::Constraint` or `::Entailment` — the only existing `Constraint` construction is `A2ARoutingResolution` (`crates/eg-types/src/decision/rapid_r044.rs`, EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R044.1), which is not wired into the general `Decide`/assemble chokepoint. Add: (a) a constraint-only short-circuit in `conclude.rs` that returns `ResolutionKind::Constraint` when hard constraints alone determine the answer, before solving/statistics run; (b) an ontology-entailment path that returns `ResolutionKind::Entailment` when the answer follows from entailment alone (reusing the existing is_a derivation machinery, EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R003). | Unit and integration tests assert `resolution_kind == Constraint` and `== Entailment` for representative cases through the production `Decide`/assemble chokepoint (not through `A2ARoutingResolution`). |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R002` | **Evidence class recorded on every decision record.** Each decision record stores an evidence_class: proof, observation or claim. The value comes from its weakest premise. Pack-provided capability annotations therefore appear as claims, not proof. | A negative test confirms a capability derived only from a pack annotation reports evidence_class claim, not proof. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R003` | **Derivation records for entailed answers.** An is_a derivation record backs an entailment-resolved answer. It names the source and premise class of each edge used. An invented or unsupported ontology edge bars the overall answer from claiming proof-level evidence. | A negative test confirms the engine refuses an answer that depends on a fabricated ontology edge. The engine does not accept it as a proof. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R004` | **Independently verifiable 0-1 integer solver certificate.** The solver in crates/eg-compute/src/solve produces a certificate naming feasibility, objective value, lower bound, optimality gap, solver version, and input digest for every solved assembly, and a separate verifier recomputes and checks that certificate independently of the solve path. | Determinism tests confirm repeated solves are byte-identical and match a committed golden certificate digest, and an exhaustive-search test confirms the solver's optimum matches brute-force search on small models. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R005` | **Conformal and risk-controlled statistical guarantees.** Statistical heads apply acceptability-set conformal prediction, selective risk control (Learn-then-Test), and adaptive conformal inference to bound miscoverage under drift, abstaining rather than returning an uncalibrated answer when these guarantees cannot be met. | Numeric unit tests fit and evaluate each guarantee and assert the resulting coverage and abstention behavior. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R006` | **Off-policy evaluation gated on effective sample size.** Decision evaluation records exact propensities and computes off-policy estimates. It uses clipped or SWITCH importance sampling and doubly robust estimators. It reports effective sample size and refuses to promote a fitted head when that size falls below the configured minimum. | A unit test asserts that off-policy evaluation reports support and effective sample size. It also verifies that the engine blocks promotion below the minimum threshold. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R007` | **Deterministic cross-target replay of committed decision records.** A committed decision record replays to byte-identical quantized fixed-point digests using a pinned software floating-point implementation. The same record verifies identically on every supported target platform. | A golden-vector replay test confirms identical digests on the supported targets. Release qualification exercises the full target matrix. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R008` | **Why-chosen and why-not explanations for solved assemblies.** Every solved assembly can explain why it chose an option. For each visible excluded option, it can explain why not. It uses a bounded re-solve for that option rather than an unbounded search. | Integration tests confirm why-not routing returns a correct, bounded explanation for excluded candidates. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R009` | **Learned ranking stays monotone with respect to safety.** A fitted statistical head may only narrow the set of legal options or cause abstention. It can never widen the legal set. It can never authorize an option that policy and hard constraints have not already permitted. | A negative test confirms that a learned head cannot override or widen the constraint-derived legal set. It also confirms that execution enforces this invariant independently of the head. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R010` | **Typed abstention reason for unmet capability.** When no legal assembly satisfies the requested capabilities, the system returns a typed AbstainReason value naming the specific missing capability rather than a generic failure. | Unit tests assert that an unsatisfiable capability request returns an AbstainReason identifying the missing capability. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R011` | **Work-budgeted search without an arbitrary result cap.** The solver in crates/eg-compute/src/solve/search bounds search by a deterministic node budget rather than an arbitrary cap on the number of candidate results, reporting an unresolved or certified-gap result when the budget is exhausted instead of silently truncating the option set. | Adversarial tests confirm that exhausting the budget above the accepted gap yields an unresolved result, that exhausting it within the accepted gap yields a certified gap, and that a parity case exhausts the budget without an incumbent. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R012` | **Shared slate crediting across multiple questions.** When multiple questions are answered against the same assembly template, the system credits outcomes at the slot level across the shared template, with results aggregated consistently across the related questions. | Template-level unit tests assert slot-level crediting is applied correctly across a shared assembly template. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R012.1` | **Slot-level crediting across questions sharing an assembly template.** When multiple questions are answered against the same assembly template, outcomes must be credited at the slot level across the shared template; today only whole- or sub-slate crediting exists. | Test: `crates/eg-types/src/decision/rapid_r012.rs::tests::credits_outcomes_at_the_slot_level_across_a_shared_assembly_template`. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R013` | **Tenant-visibility filtering before feature computation.** Decision candidates are filtered by tenant and row-level visibility before any feature is computed or counted, and no report or aggregate may expose a candidate, feature value, or statistic derived from a source outside the requesting tenant's visibility. | A negative test confirms that a candidate from another tenant is excluded from decision results and aggregates. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R014` | **Composition solved as bounded constraint optimization.** Agent or component composition is resolved by lowering covering, implication, exactly-one, knapsack, feature-compatibility, and cardinality requirements into a bounded 0-1 integer program and solving it exactly, rather than by a single unverified model-generated choice. | Exhaustive-search and model-validation tests confirm the lowered constraint model produces correct, verifiable compositions across representative cases. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R015` | **No generative model weights inside the decision engine (rollup).** The decision engine contains no large language model weights and performs no free-text generation. Its statistical heads are small fitted models used only to rank legal options or abstain. Split into child requirements by code root. `.1` is a typed, closed StatisticalHeadKind refusing a non-fitted-head name. `.2` wires the ladder's head registry through the typed head-kind parse. | Architecture and dependency review confirms no generative model weights or text-generation calls are present in the decision engine's code paths. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R015.1` | **Typed, closed StatisticalHeadKind refusing a non-fitted-head name.** Add the typed, closed `StatisticalHeadKind::parse` (`crates/eg-types/src/decision/rapid_r015.rs`). It refuses any head-kind name outside the two fitted heads the ladder uses, including a generative-model label. | Test: `crates/eg-types/src/decision/rapid_r015.rs::tests::refuses_a_generative_model_name`. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R015.2` | **Wire the ladder's head registry through the typed head-kind parse.** Remaining scope from the split (2026-10-09): wiring the ladder's head registry to construct heads only through this parse. Split into grandchild requirements (2026-10-09). `.1` a total, reversible mapping between the closed `StatisticalHeadKind` and the ladder's production `HeadKind` registry, `.2` routing `DecisionFitRequest` construction through that mapping at admission. | Architecture and dependency review confirms no generative model weights or text-generation calls are present in the decision engine's code paths. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R015.2.1` | **Total, reversible mapping between StatisticalHeadKind and the production HeadKind registry.** Add `StatisticalHeadKind::to_head_kind` and `StatisticalHeadKind::from_head_kind` (`crates/eg-types/src/decision/rapid_r015.rs`), connecting the closed, parse-validated name set to the ladder's actual `HeadKind` enum, with the resident-scorer `OptionAttention` variant correctly outside the closed set rather than silently coerced into it. | Test: `crates/eg-types/src/decision/rapid_r015.rs::tests::wires_the_production_head_kind_through_the_closed_registry`. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R015.2.2` | **Route DecisionFitRequest construction through the typed mapping.** Remaining scope: validate `DecisionFitRequest.head_kind` through `StatisticalHeadKind::from_head_kind` at admission (`crates/eg-types/src/decision/jobs.rs`), deciding how an `OptionAttention` fit request is admitted given it is outside the `.1`/`.2.1` closed set. | Architecture and dependency review confirms no generative model weights or text-generation calls are present in the decision engine's code paths. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R016` | **Training restricted to independently evaluated outcomes.** Calibration and training data include only outcomes recorded with exact propensities by approved commit principals; a self-reported or language-model-resolved outcome is excluded from the calibration or training set. | Unit tests confirm that every planted defective label is refused by name and that a language-model-resolved abstention never enters the calibration set. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R017` | **Single write authority per durable decision state kind.** Each durable decision-state kind has exactly one write authority responsible for committing it. Examples include an assembled agent graph, a graph mutation batch, or a published component with its receipt. No second code path may independently mutate that state. | Integration tests confirm only the designated path commits each state kind. They also confirm bypass attempts fail. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R018` | **Unknown facts never default to zero in the solver.** When a cost, latency, or capability fact needed by the solver objective is unknown, the solver treats it as unknown rather than silently substituting zero or a true or false default, and surfaces the gap instead of producing a misleadingly optimal result. | A unit test confirms that an unknown required fact prevents a false-confidence solve rather than being silently defaulted. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R019` | **Decision commit re-derives and verifies before writing.** Committing a decision result re-derives the result, verifies its certificate, checks every pinned component revision, and compares the candidate-universe catalog digest with a compare-and-set, all within one transaction, so a stale catalog or fabricated input fails closed instead of being committed. | Integration tests confirm that a stale catalog or a fabricated input is rejected at commit time while a byte-identical retry commits idempotently. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R020` | **Bounded, keyed exploration excluded from sensitive questions.** Exploratory ranking requires an explicit budget and an unpredictable HMAC-keyed seed, and is unavailable for security, policy, write-back, and irreversible-action questions regardless of the requested budget. | Unit tests verify the keyed seed against a published HMAC test vector and confirm that exploration on a security question is refused by the served path. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R021` | **Versioned feature schema for decision candidates.** Decision ranking computes a versioned feature set over caller-visible candidates, including vector similarity, candidate-local BM25, entailed coverage, provenance quality, belief confidence, cost and latency, freshness, and outcome statistics, so a feature schema change can be tracked and replayed. | Unit and client tests assert the versioned feature vector is computed correctly for representative candidates. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R022` | **Separation of full-label and bandit-label training regimes.** Training data distinguishes gold-labelled questions, which provide a full label, from executed-option-only outcomes, which provide a bandit label, and each regime is admitted into fitting only through its own path. | Unit tests in the admission path confirm gold-labelled and bandit-labelled data are routed and validated separately. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R023` | **Hierarchical pooling of outcome statistics with a minimum-sample gate.** Pool outcome statistics hierarchically by kind, capability class and option using a Beta-Binomial model. A pool below its configured sample minimum cannot support fitted-head promotion. | A unit test confirms insufficient pooled support for an option blocks promotion. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R024` | **Multi-class and binary calibration utilities.** Decision confidence uses temperature, vector, or Dirichlet scaling for multi-class outputs. It uses isotonic calibration only for the binary entity-resolution case. The engine records the calibration method on the resulting receipt. | A unit test confirms pooled calibration output is recorded on the fit receipt. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R025` | **Drift detection via out-of-distribution checks and adaptive conformal bounds.** The decision engine detects drift by checking incoming features for out-of-distribution values and by bounding miscoverage with adaptive conformal inference, abstaining rather than ranking when drift is detected. | A unit test confirms that an out-of-distribution feature causes the head to abstain. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R026` | **Inverse-probability-weighted audit sampling of acted decisions.** A sample of acted decisions is selected for audit using inverse-probability weighting at a fixed five-percent rate, and every audited decision record carries an audit flag usable by downstream calibration. | Unit tests assert that evaluated records carry the audit flag and that calibration correctly applies the inverse-probability weighting. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R026.1` | **Calibration-weighting half of audit sampling is tested in-crate.** The audit-flag sampling half of inverse-probability-weighted audit sampling has a bound test, but the calibration-weighting half (`pub(crate) audit_weight`/targets) has no in-crate `#[cfg(test)]` test confirming calibration correctly applies the inverse-probability weight. | Test: `crates/eg-numeric/src/decision/audit.rs::tests::calibration_applies_the_pub_crate_inverse_probability_audit_weight`. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R027` | **WeightedFeatures and ListwiseLogistic fitted decision heads.** The decision engine supports a WeightedFeatures head, which is advisory only and never calibrated, and a ListwiseLogistic head, which is a deterministic, bit-for-bit reproducible optimizer; a further Kolmogorov-Arnold listwise head is not yet included. | Unit tests confirm a fit is bit-for-bit reproducible and certifies an act threshold, and that the WeightedFeatures head remains advisory and is never calibrated. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R028` | **Natural-language template routing without a generative model.** A natural-language decision request is routed to a matching NlTemplate using candidate-local BM25, embedding, or ontology-label similarity rather than a generative model call. | Unit tests cover natural-language template matching; end-to-end served routing is proven when the serving path is exercised. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R029` | **Decision-based retrieval-plan selection.** The decision ladder selects among retrieval plans, such as LeanRAG, reciprocal-rank fusion, or direct SPARQL querying, for a given request using the same typed decision mechanism as other question families. | An integration test asserts the correct retrieval plan is selected for representative request shapes. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R030` | **Decision-based ingestion path routing.** Ingestion requests are routed to a fast, medium, or slow processing path through the same typed decision mechanism used for other question families, rather than a hard-coded rule. | An integration test confirms ingestion routing selects the expected path for representative inputs. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R031` | **Expected-value scheduling for enrichment work.** Enrichment scheduling selects candidate work by comparing expected value against cost through the typed decision mechanism, rather than a fixed priority order. | An integration test confirms enrichment scheduling selects the higher expected-value option under representative cost scenarios. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R032` | **Entity-resolution candidates with conformal guarantees (rollup).** Entity-resolution (owl:sameAs) candidate decisions use the binary entity-matching head with isotonic calibration and report a conformal guarantee validated against a gold label set, abstaining when that guarantee cannot be met. Split (2026-10-09): `.1` the declared-but-unused QuestionKind, `.2` the full ResolveEntity decision path. | A unit test against a gold-labelled set confirms the reported conformal guarantee holds and that insufficient confidence results in abstention. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R032.1` | **`QuestionKind::ResolveEntity` is declared.** `QuestionKind::ResolveEntity` is declared in `crates/eg-types/src/decision/statistical.rs`. No code constructs, matches, or tests it anywhere in the repo. There is no entity-matching head, isotonic calibration, or conformal-guarantee code for this kind. | Compiles as part of the `QuestionKind` enum. No test exists (none is claimed). |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R032.2` | **Remaining scope: ResolveEntity decision path with conformal guarantee.** Build the full path: a binary entity-matching head trained/scored over owl:sameAs candidate pairs, isotonic calibration of its score, a conformal guarantee computed against a gold-labelled set, and abstention when the guarantee cannot be met — dispatched through `Decide` on `QuestionKind::ResolveEntity`. | A unit test against a gold-labelled set confirms the reported conformal guarantee holds and that insufficient confidence results in abstention, exercised through `QuestionKind::ResolveEntity` end to end. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R033` | **Decision-based schema mapping for SQL sources (rollup).** Mapping a source column to an ontology property for SQL ingestion and pack content is resolved through the typed decision mechanism, producing a proposed mapping with its evidence class rather than an unverified direct assignment. Split (2026-10-09): `.1` the declared-but-unused QuestionKind, `.2` the full SchemaMapping decision path. | An integration test confirms a representative column set maps to the expected ontology properties with correct evidence classification. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R033.1` | **`QuestionKind::SchemaMapping` is declared.** `QuestionKind::SchemaMapping` is declared in `crates/eg-types/src/decision/statistical.rs`. No code constructs, matches, or tests it anywhere in the repo; there is no SQL-column-to-ontology-property decision path. | Compiles as part of the `QuestionKind` enum; no test exists (none is claimed). |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R033.2` | **Remaining scope: SchemaMapping decision path for SQL ingestion.** Build the full path: given a SQL source column (name, type, sample content) and the ontology's candidate properties, resolve the mapping through `Decide` on `QuestionKind::SchemaMapping`, producing a proposed mapping and its evidence class (proof/observation/claim) rather than an unverified direct assignment, for both SQL ingestion and pack content. | An integration test confirms a representative column set maps to the expected ontology properties with correct evidence classification, exercised through `QuestionKind::SchemaMapping` end to end. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R034` | **Proposal-only contradiction-handling suggestions.** When the truth-maintenance system detects a contradiction, the decision engine may propose a resolution but never performs an automatic retraction. Any retraction requires a separate, explicit authorization step. | A negative test confirms that a detected contradiction produces a proposal only and that no automatic retraction occurs. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R035` | **Decision-based routing for execution configuration.** This repository's graph.decide() method accepts requests to select among candidate models, prompts, skills, tools, harness modes, account modes, or sandbox configurations and returns a ranked or constrained choice with its evidence class; the consuming execution environment applies that choice. | A contract test confirms graph.decide() returns a valid typed choice and evidence class for each supported routing category. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R036` | **graph.assemble() as the authority for agent composition.** graph.assemble() is the sole method for composing an agent graph from typed tasks and capabilities, replacing a model-generated composition choice with a verifiable constraint-solved result. | An integration test confirms graph.assemble() produces a legal, certificate-backed agent graph for representative composition requests. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R037` | **Escalated abstentions re-enter as claims.** When a typed abstention is escalated to a language model or a human reviewer and a resolution is produced, that resolution re-enters the decision engine only as a claim-class input, never as a proof or observation. | A negative test confirms a resolution produced by escalation is recorded with evidence_class claim. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R038` | **Advisory pre-tool risk scoring.** Before a tool invocation, the decision engine can return an advisory risk score for the candidate action. The score informs but never itself authorizes or blocks execution. | A unit test confirms the risk score is returned as advisory and does not gate execution on its own. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R038.1` | **Typed ToolInvocation / pre-tool risk concept exists.** No risk/pretool/ToolInvocation concept exists in the engine. Add the typed representation a caller can score before dispatching a tool invocation, advisory only. | Test: `crates/eg-types/src/decision/rapid_r038.rs::tests::returns_an_advisory_risk_score_for_a_candidate_tool_invocation_without_gating_execution`. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R039` | **Cost-aware routing using accounting data (rollup).** Routing decisions incorporate cost information sourced from the usage and cost accounting subsystem, preferring lower-cost legal options when they satisfy the same constraints. Split (2026-10-09): `.1` the missing-data abstention path that landed, `.2` the missing cost-preference ranking. | An integration test confirms a routing decision prefers the lower-cost legal option when accounting data distinguishes candidates. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R039.1` | **Routing abstains naming the fact when L5 accounting data is missing.** `cost_routing_without_l5_accounting_abstains_naming_the_fact` (EH-039, `src/server/handlers/decide/stat_tests/consumer_tests.rs`) confirms that when there is no L5 cost-accounting data for the candidates, the routing decision abstains and names the missing-data fact rather than guessing. | Test: `src/server/handlers/decide/stat_tests/consumer_tests.rs::cost_routing_without_l5_accounting_abstains_naming_the_fact`. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R039.2` | **Remaining scope: cost-preference ranking when accounting data is present.** No production code path ranks or prefers options by a cost-like feature when L5 accounting data is present — this needs a published scoring head over observed/accounted cost (consuming `ObservedCost`, see AU-CONTROL-R006) feeding the deterministic ladder, since the exploration policy currently samples uniformly over legal options rather than preferring the cheaper one. | An integration test confirms a routing decision prefers the lower-cost legal option when accounting data distinguishes candidates (the original R039 acceptance test, now bound to this child). |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R040` | **Guarded promotion of fitted heads via publish receipt.** A fitted decision head is promoted to active use only through the component-publish path gated on a matching DecisionEval receipt. A receipt that does not match the evaluated digest causes promotion to be refused. | Unit tests confirm a mismatched receipt is refused with an evaluation-receipt-mismatch error while a matching receipt allows publish. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R041` | **Connector event triage through Decide.** EG classifies inbound connector events by routing them through the decision ladder in evaluate-only mode, returning a proposal and its evidence class without taking any action on the connector's behalf. A sampled subset of these evaluations is logged for audit. | An integration test submits synthetic connector events, calls the evaluate-only triage path, and asserts no side effect occurs while a sampled fraction of evaluations is recorded. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R041.1` | **Connector-event evaluate-only triage path with sampled audit logging.** No connector-event evaluate-only triage path or sampled-audit mechanism exists. Inbound connector events must be classified via the decision ladder in evaluate-only mode with a sampled fraction logged for audit. | Test: `src/server/handlers/decide/stat_tests/consumer_tests.rs::connector_event_triage_is_evaluate_only_and_logs_a_sampled_fraction_for_audit`. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R042` | **Connector tool-choice advisory via Decide.** Decide evaluates which internal tool a connector should use for a given task and returns a proposal with evidence only; the connector's own deterministic authorization path, not Decide, governs whether any resulting write-back actually occurs. | A test exercises tool-choice evaluation for a connector task and confirms the proposal alone cannot trigger a write-back without passing the separate deterministic authorization check. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R043` | **Write-back proposals routed through Decide.** EG's decision ladder can produce a write-back proposal with supporting evidence for a connector action. The proposal never authorizes the write itself. Any exploration or statistical head that generates the proposal stays outside the authorization step. That deterministic step must separately approve the write. | A negative test asserts a Decide-sourced write-back proposal has no external effect. The effect occurs only after the connector's own deterministic authorization check passes. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R044` | **A2A task routing through the decision ladder (rollup).** Inbound A2A tasks are routed to an existing or assembled agent graph. Routing applies the same typed decision ladder used for other question families. It returns a routing resolution with its resolution kind and evidence class rather than an unexplained match. Split into child requirements by code root. `.1` typed A2ARoutingResolution refusal over ResolutionKind/EvidenceClass, `.2` wire A2A task submission through the typed routing resolution. | An integration test submits inbound A2A tasks and asserts each is routed to a graph through a recorded decision with a resolution kind and evidence class. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R044.1` | **Typed A2ARoutingResolution refusal over ResolutionKind/EvidenceClass.** Add the typed `A2ARoutingResolution::new` refusal (`crates/eg-types/src/decision/rapid_r044.rs`) over the existing `ResolutionKind`/`EvidenceClass`, refusing an empty graph id and an abstention claiming proof-grade evidence. | Test: `crates/eg-types/src/decision/rapid_r044.rs::tests::refuses_an_abstention_claiming_proof_grade_evidence`. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R044.2` | **Wire A2A task submission through the typed routing resolution.** Remaining scope from the split (2026-10-09): the A2A task-submission entry point that constructs one of these per inbound task and an integration test over the served path. Split into grandchild requirements (2026-10-09): `.1` a typed registry-lookup routing function over known graph ids, `.2` the served A2A task-submission entry point calling it with an integration test. | An integration test submits inbound A2A tasks and asserts each is routed to a graph through a recorded decision with a resolution kind and evidence class. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R044.2.1` | **Typed registry-lookup routing function over known graph ids.** Add `A2ARoutingResolution::route` (`crates/eg-types/src/decision/rapid_r044.rs`): given the known graph-id registry and a requested id, resolves to the matching graph as a Constraint/Observation resolution or abstains as Claim-grade evidence, always through `::new`. | Test: `crates/eg-types/src/decision/rapid_r044.rs::tests::routes_to_a_known_graph_and_abstains_otherwise`. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R044.2.2` | **Served A2A task-submission entry point.** Remaining scope: the actual inbound A2A task-submission handler that builds the known-graph registry per request and calls `A2ARoutingResolution::route`, with an integration test over the served path. | An integration test submits inbound A2A tasks and asserts each is routed to a graph through a recorded decision with a resolution kind and evidence class. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R045` | **Smallest covering tool subset within a context budget.** Decide selects the smallest tool subset that still covers every required capability within a caller-specified context budget, so a multiplexer can expose fewer tools to a caller without losing coverage. | A test constructs a capability requirement set with a token budget and asserts the returned subset is minimal, covers every required capability, and stays within budget. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R045.1` | **Typed tool-subset request and greedy covering-set selection.** EG defines a typed tool-subset request body and a greedy set-cover selection over it, with no server or candidate-source dependency. | Unit tests assert the selection is minimal, covers every required capability, and refuses when coverage or budget cannot be met. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R045.2` | **Tool-subset selection wired into Decide.** EG wires the R045.1 selection into a dedicated Decide question kind, reading tool candidates from the agent library and returning the selected subset on the served decision record. | A served test submits a tool-subset question and asserts the returned record's selected subset is minimal, covers every required capability, and stays within the caller's budget. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R046` | **Swarm topology decisions via the decision ladder.** EG answers swarm-topology questions by applying its typed decision ladder to capacity and shape constraints, returning a proposed topology or a typed abstention together with its evidence; EG itself never acquires or leases capacity as part of answering the question. | A test exercises topology decision requests and asserts a constraint-checked proposal or abstention is returned with no capacity lease taken before separate acquisition. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R047` | **DecisionRecord v1 contract: types and methods.** EG defines versioned DecisionRecord v1 types, the AgentAssemble and DecisionCommit methods, a DecisionRecord component kind, and a decide feature flag, introduced through a single count-pinned schema change so generated contracts stay consistent across languages. | A contract-generation test confirms the Rust, Python, and wire descriptors for AgentAssemble and DecisionCommit are generated, fresh, and each has exactly one reachable handler. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R048` | **Entailment derivation with weakest-premise evidence class.** EG records is_a derivation entries for task-to-capability closure and assigns each derivation the weakest premise evidence class among its inputs, and an independent verifier re-checks claim-premised task mappings against those derivations. | Unit tests build derivation chains with mixed premise classes and assert the verifier recomputes the same weakest-premise conclusion. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R049` | **Exact 0-1 solver with certificate and verifier.** EG solves legal assemblies with a bounded 0-1 integer program. The `verify_certificate` routine and brute-force oracle check it. The solver also supports template enumeration and no-good-cut re-solves. | Solver tests compare optima and certificates with exhaustive enumeration on small instances. They confirm `verify_certificate` rejects corrupted certificates. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R050` | **Typed optional model and tool facts.** EG represents model and tool facts such as modality, cost, latency, and schema digests as typed optional fields on the component schema, and the unread provides field is removed. | A schema test confirms provides no longer exists and a stored-component test round-trips the typed optional facts. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R051` | **AgentAssemble and DecisionCommit handlers with audit lines.** EG's AgentAssemble and DecisionCommit handlers enforce one agent graph per commit, refuse any pack-provided DecisionRecord component, and emit an audit line for each commit outcome. | Handler tests cover a successful commit, a rejected pack-sourced DecisionRecord, a fabricated record, a cross-tenant record, and retired, withdrawn, or denied commit attempts. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R052` | **Routing acceptance fixture with golden vectors.** EG ships a routing acceptance fixture. | `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R054` | **Statistical contract: Decide, DecisionFit, DecisionEval.** EG defines the Decide, DecisionFit, and DecisionEval methods together with a DecisionRecord v2 schema, and generates the corresponding TTL and SHACL ontology artifacts from that contract. | A contract-surface test confirms all three methods are served and listed, and a generation check confirms the TTL and SHACL files match the contract. |

| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R079` | **Per-unit embedding admission classifier for ingestion.** Ingestion applies a deterministic per-unit classifier before embedding any derived text: it admits prose, document, and comment content while excluding generated, lockfile, vendored, and minified content, skips embedding when an exact, BM25, or graph lookup already answers the content, deduplicates near-identical text by entropy and content hash, enforces size bounds, and for SQL columns embeds only high-cardinality free text rather than enumerations, foreign keys, timestamps, booleans, or numeric columns. | `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R079.1` | **Typed content-class taxonomy and pure admission gate (producer slice of R079).** A closed `ContentClass` enum fixes the taxonomy -- prose, document, and comment as eligible; generated, lockfile, vendored, and minified as excluded -- behind a single pure `admits()` gate, so every later ingestion call site consults one deterministic registry instead of an ad hoc per-site check. Real ingestion wiring, the embedding client, cheaper-index skip, entropy/hash dedup, size bounds, SQL-column cardinality, and retrieval-telemetry feedback are out of scope for this slice and remain open under `R079`. | A unit test feeds each excluded content class and asserts `admits() == false`, and feeds each eligible class and asserts `admits() == true`. |

| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R079.3` | **Duplicate and size admission.** Add the served fixture in `tests/test_decision_ingestion_acceptance.py` proving entropy/content-hash deduplication and size bounds before an embedding call. | `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R079.4` | **SQL column admission.** Add the served fixture in `tests/test_decision_ingestion_acceptance.py` proving that ingestion admits high-cardinality free-text SQL columns and excludes enumerations, foreign keys, timestamps, booleans, and numeric columns. | Planned `tests/test_decision_ingestion_acceptance.py::test_sql_column_kinds_and_cardinality_control_embedding`; not implemented or executed acceptance evidence. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R079.5` | **Retrieval feedback admission.** Add the served fixture in `tests/test_decision_ingestion_acceptance.py` proving that retrieval-use telemetry demotes never-retrieved content classes at the ingestion admission path. | `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R080` | **Ingestion cost ladder with provenance rungs.** Ingestion derives each fact through the cheapest sufficient rung of a six-level cost ladder covering AST extraction, symbol or type resolution, statistical or community derivation, classical machine-learning or named-entity modelling, embeddings, and finally a language model, tagging each fact with its rung's provenance class. A higher rung runs only when every lower rung abstains, and no rung may overwrite a fact a lower, more deterministic rung already established. Split producer-first into `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R080.1` (the typed model); further child slices wire in the real per-rung extractors. | A ladder test seeds inputs answerable at each rung and asserts the fact is produced by the lowest sufficient rung with the matching provenance tag, and that a higher rung never overwrites an existing deterministic fact. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R080.1` | **Typed provenance-rung model and overwrite-protection rule.** `crates/eg-types/src/decision/ingestion_ladder.rs` defines the six-level `ProvenanceRung` enum (cheapest first) and a pure `resolve_rung` function: a candidate fact from a higher (more expensive) rung never overwrites a fact a lower rung already established, and between two candidates the lowest-rung one wins. The real per-rung extractors (AST walking, symbol resolution, embeddings, an LLM call) are out of scope for this slice. | A unit test resolves a deterministic fact against a higher-rung guess and asserts the deterministic fact survives, and resolves a higher-rung guess against a later lower-rung fact and asserts the lower-rung fact replaces it. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R080.2` | **Served enrichment ladder qualification.** Add the served fixture in `tests/test_decision_ingestion_acceptance.py` against the existing ingestion and enrichment entry points. For each of the six parent rungs, require every cheaper rung to abstain before escalation, persist the actual producing rung, and refuse overwrite of a cheaper established fact. The producer-only `resolve_rung` test does not discharge this integration acceptance. | Planned `tests/test_decision_ingestion_acceptance.py::test_real_extractors_follow_the_lowest_sufficient_rung`; not implemented or executed acceptance evidence. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R081` | **Confidence-weighted community detection excluding similarity edges.** Community detection computes edge weights from the entity resolver's confidence score, so a scoped match binds a community more strongly than a unique-guess match, instead of leaving every edge unweighted. The structural adjacency used to detect communities excludes, or heavily discounts, similar_to resemblance edges, since two code shapes looking alike is not a structural relationship. | A unit test compares community assignments across inputs with varying resolver confidence and asserts that adding similar_to edges does not change community membership. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R082` | **Single-pass option-marker scoring head for Decide.** The eg-decision scoring head encodes the decision state once and scores every candidate option at its own marker position within that single forward pass, applying softmax across the option-marker outputs in-process rather than iterating a decoder once per option. | A unit test issues one encoder pass over more than one option and asserts all option scores are returned together and sum to one under softmax, with no per-option re-encoding. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R083` | **Options encode structured facts, not option-label text (rollup).** The decision scorer encodes each candidate option from the structured facts attached to its IRI, including SHACL shape, declared capabilities, and cost and latency facts, rather than from a serialized text label, so the encoded feature width is bounded by a declared cardinality instead of a token budget. Split into child requirements by code root: `.1` typed OptionFacts encoding bounded by declared capability cardinality, `.2` wire OptionFacts encoding into the real FeatureMatrix computation. | A test scores an option set large enough to exceed a plausible text-token budget and asserts scoring still succeeds because feature width is bounded by cardinality, not text length. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R083.1` | **Typed OptionFacts encoding bounded by declared capability cardinality.** Add the typed `OptionFacts::encode` (`crates/eg-numeric/src/decision/rapid_r083.rs`), bounded by the declared `MAX_OPTION_CAPABILITIES` cardinality rather than by any text-token budget. | Test: `crates/eg-numeric/src/decision/rapid_r083.rs::tests::scores_an_option_whose_capability_text_would_exceed_a_token_budget`. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R083.2` | **Wire OptionFacts encoding into the real FeatureMatrix computation.** Remaining scope from the split (2026-10-09): wiring this encoding into the real `FeatureMatrix` computation in `decision::features`. | A test scores an option set large enough to exceed a plausible text-token budget and asserts scoring still succeeds because feature width is bounded by cardinality, not text length. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R084` | **Conformal calibration as primary rung over temperature scaling.** Decide calibrates confidence using eg-numeric's conformal prediction as the primary mechanism, reporting distribution-free empirical coverage and a principled abstention threshold, and falls back to size-specific temperature scaling only when the exchangeability assumption conformal coverage needs does not hold. Every calibrated decision reports both expected calibration error and empirical coverage. | An integration test measures expected calibration error and empirical coverage across option sets of varying size and asserts the declared coverage target holds without a size-specific temperature parameter. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R084.1` | **ECE/coverage measurement across option sets of varying size.** No expected-calibration-error/coverage measurement exists across option sets of varying size. Fixtures are fixed at 3 options, so the declared coverage-holds-without-a-size-specific-parameter claim is untested. | Test: `tests/decision/calibration_coverage.rs::measures_ece_and_empirical_coverage_across_option_sets_of_varying_size`. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R085` | **Legal option sets are derived from the ontology, never received as-is.** EG derives the candidate option set from SHACL shapes and a bounded SPARQL shortlist query rather than accepting a caller-supplied option list, so only type-valid, policy-admissible candidates are ever scored. A learned ranking rung may narrow this set or abstain but can never add an option the derivation excluded. | A negative test supplies a caller-asserted option that violates a SHACL shape or policy constraint and asserts it never appears in the derived option set presented to the scorer. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R085.1` | **Typed legal-option-set filter (producer-first split of R085).** A pure, typed filter derives the legal option set from a caller-supplied candidate list and an admissibility predicate, excluding every candidate the predicate rejects, as the type-level contract ahead of the real SHACL-shape / SPARQL-shortlist-backed predicate (`R085.2`). | A negative test constructs a candidate set containing one option that fails a fixture admissibility predicate and asserts it is excluded from the derived set, plus a positive case that an admissible option passes through. |

| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R086` | **DecisionFit promotion protocol reports nine metrics.** Before a fitted head may be promoted, DecisionEval reports accuracy, soft accuracy, Brier score and score mean absolute error alongside empirical coverage paired with expected calibration error, abstention rate paired with accuracy on answered decisions, cost per decision, decision stability where the same state and option set always produce the same answer, and calibration drift measured over bitemporal history, computed per tenant and per domain over a fixed evaluation workflow set. | A promotion-gate test asserts a receipt missing any of the nine metrics, or showing decision instability for a repeated identical request, blocks promotion. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R087` | **Scorer always receives a bounded shortlist, never a full catalog.** EG always shortlists candidates with a bounded SPARQL LIMIT before handing an option set to the statistical scorer, so the scorer never receives an unbounded catalog of candidates. | A test against a large candidate catalog asserts the scorer's input option set never exceeds the declared shortlist bound. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R088` | **Belief-as-of-t exposed as a Decide primitive over prefixes.** Decide exposes a primitive that computes a belief or probability as of a given point in a sequence, such as a conversation prefix, using the existing bitemporal AS OF query over temporal slices rather than a bespoke per-domain sequential model. | A unit test evaluates belief as of successive prefixes of a recorded sequence and asserts the results match direct bitemporal AS OF queries at the corresponding points. |

| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R089` | **Learned ranking never replaces the constraint-first decision ladder.** A statistical rung in the decision ladder may only rank or narrow the option set that the constraint, entailment and optimization steps have already produced, or abstain; it is never the mechanism that determines which options are legal. | A test disables the statistical scorer and asserts the constraint, entailment and optimization steps still produce the identical legal option set, with only ranking or abstention behavior affected. |

| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R091` | **Resident scorer is bit-reproducible via fixed-point arithmetic.** The resident decision-scorer head computes entirely in fixed-point integer arithmetic, following eg-numeric's no-floating-point-transcendental discipline, so scoring the same recorded decision input reproduces byte-identical results across machines and over time rather than only remaining stable within a single run. | A golden-vector test replays a committed DecisionRecord on a different machine or build and asserts the recomputed score is byte-identical to the recorded value. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R092` | **Scorer selection and structured encoding apply ladder-wide.** Across every decision question, the statistical scorer selects only among options the deterministic constraint, entailment and optimization rungs have already derived and can never introduce a new option, and every option is encoded from its structured ontology features rather than natural-language text, so scoring is not limited by option-label language or length. | A cross-question test exercises two distinct decision question types and asserts both enforce select-only-from-derived-options behavior and structured, non-text option encoding. |

Outcomes include `invalid_measurement`, `missing_required_fact`, `no_admissible_package`, `stale_source`, `work_budget_exhausted` and `certificate_unacceptable`. They carry no certified package. <!-- spec-definition-patch:comparative-analysis-r139 -->
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R139` | **Comparative-analysis plan producer (rollup).** EG emits a revision-pinned plan for zero-model vector discovery, selective synthesis in one or two model-service calls, and high-weight-only deep extraction. Every stage has an explicit call budget and the plan has a total ceiling. EG returns a plan and premises; AU owns generation and the separate invocation receipt. R080.1 and R136.3 are adjacent reusable mechanisms, not comparative-analysis coverage. | Served `Method::Decide` returns the plan with zero model calls during discovery; AU invokes only budgeted synthesis/extraction. Stale inputs and exhausted budgets refuse before a later call. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R139.1` | **Model.** Define `ComparativeAnalysisRequest`, `ComparativeCandidate{candidate_id, candidate_revision, score, importance}`, `ComparativeAnalysisStage`, `ComparativeStageBudget`, `ComparativeAnalysisPlan`, `ComparativeAnalysisRefusal` and `ComparativeAnalysisEvidence`; add `QuestionKind::ComparativeAnalysisPlan` serialized as `comparative_analysis_plan`. Define `ComparativeAnalysisPlan` fields `plan_digest`, `tenant_id`, `space_id`, `space_revision`, `query_vector_digest`, `unified_query_result_digest`, `candidates`, `policy_version`, `synthesis_importance_threshold`, `extraction_importance_threshold`, `stage_budgets` and `total_call_ceiling`. Each ordered candidate has `candidate_id`, `candidate_revision`, `score` and `importance`. Each ordered stage budget has `stage` and `max_calls`. Reuse the existing 64-candidate decision limit. The EG plan contains no generated text or AU execution receipt. | Round-trip every binding, candidate, threshold, stage budget and question kind; reject malformed, duplicate and over-limit candidates. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R139.2` | **Behavior.** Reuse only caller-supplied, revision-pinned vectors and completed `UnifiedQuery` results; discovery makes exactly zero model-service calls and does not synthesize embeddings. Preserve visible-candidate stable order. Versioned defaults select synthesis candidates at normalized importance `>= 0.50` and extraction candidates at `>= 0.80`. Budgets are discovery `0`, synthesis `1` or `2`, extraction `0–64`; stage sums must fit an explicit total ceiling. The AU caller must not retry implicitly or issue an unbudgeted subcall. | Prove zero-call discovery, inclusive thresholds, stable ties, one/two-call synthesis, high-weight-only extraction, and refusal before it crosses a stage or total budget. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R139.3` | **Wiring and authority.** Add the question to public `Method::Decide`: wire dispatch -> `src/server/handlers/decide/statistical.rs::handle_decide` -> `src/server/handlers/decide/stat_decide.rs::handle_decide`. EG validates pinned inputs and returns only a plan/refusal; it never invokes a model or treats AU output as EG-produced. AU may invoke only after plan receipt, at its existing `agent_utilities/knowledge_graph/retrieval/context_compiler_serving.py::compiled_chat_completion` boundary. `AU-HARNESS-R024.3` trace digests do not establish a comparative-analysis consumer contract. | Actual Decide dispatch returns a plan without calling a model transport. Current AU main defines no R139 consumer or receipt at this boundary. The pending dependency is reserved as `AU-SEMANTIC-R030` and `.1`–`.6` in `specs/au-semantic-client`; its owner must define and serve-test it. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R139.4` | **Typed refusal.** Before returning a consumable plan, refuse missing/stale query inputs or candidate revisions; invisible, malformed, duplicate or over-limit candidates; importance outside `[0,1]`; invalid threshold ordering; absent/invalid stage budgets; and stage sums above total. A stage that cannot fit its remaining ceiling refuses with stage, requested and remaining calls. Never clamp, return a partial plan or make a model call on refusal. | Served negative cases cover stale bindings, invalid values, and each stage/total exhaustion; observe typed refusal and zero model calls. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R139.5` | **Evidence.** Bind plan digest to tenant/space revision, query-vector/result digests, exact visible candidate IDs/scores/weights/order, policy and thresholds, and stage/total budgets. The proposed AU receipt fields are `plan_digest`, `run_id`, `caller_id`, `actual_stage_calls` (ordered `{stage, call_count}` entries), `request_digest`, `result_digest`, `status` (`completed` or `refused`) and `refusal_code` (refusal only; `result_digest` is absent on refusal). AU validates each stage and total ceiling. EG records premises and plan only and cannot mint or attest this receipt. | Mutating a bound input changes the plan digest; AU receipt validation rejects a mismatched plan or calls above budget. EG stores no generated output and makes no claim about AU execution evidence. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R139.6` | **Served acceptance.** Exercise the authenticated EG `Method::Decide` producer positively and with stale/over-budget refusals, then exercise the separate served AU consumer receiving the plan and invoking its configured service only within synthesis/extraction budgets. Use the real adapters, not only the pure planner. Preserve R080.1/R136.3 as adjacent mechanisms. | EG: `tests/decide/test_comparative_analysis_served.py::test_plan_and_refusal_through_served_decide`. AU: `tests/decide/test_comparative_analysis_consumer.py::test_served_consumer_obeys_plan_and_binds_invocation_receipt`. The AU consumer and receipt remain pending the reserved `AU-SEMANTIC-R030` definition and owner-served test. Both exact source SHAs and receipts are required for an end-to-end claim. |
<!-- /spec-definition-patch -->

| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R100.101` | **Grounded topic coverage and probability-scored unsupported claims.** Extend `DecisionEval` with `GroundedAnswerSubmit` and `GroundedAnswerEvaluationV1`. Bind corpus/query/answer/claim/citation revisions and digests. Record scope (`single_document`, `local_multi_document`, `global_corpus`), evaluator/policy revisions and independent label-set revision/digest. Each query carries a nonempty `ExpectedTopicSetV1` of unique required topic ids. For each expected topic, bind answer claim ids mapped to it and per-claim/per-citation support votes. A topic is covered only when a mapped answer claim cites a source labeled supporting. Its valid support-vote fraction must meet `minimum_topic_support_probability`. Fewer than `minimum_valid_votes` refuses scoring. Return exact `expected_topic_ids` and `covered_topic_ids`. Per scope, coverage is `sum(|covered_topic_ids|) / sum(|expected_topic_ids|)` over the complete pinned query set, not an average of per-query ratios. Require exactly one `GroundedClaimPredictionV1` per scored claim. Bind claim/answer/input digests and pinned scorer id/revision/artifact digest. The scorer cannot access evaluation labels. It supplies fixed-point predicted unsupported probability `p_i` in [0,1]. Independently labeled votes bind unique annotator refs and `unsupported_votes`/`supported_votes`. Set target `q_i = unsupported_votes / valid_votes_i`. `C` is the complete required claim set. Each claim needs exactly one prediction and at least one valid independent vote. Missing/extra claims or predictions, duplicate annotator votes, empty `C`, or zero total vote weight refuses before scoring. Report weighted soft-label Brier score `sum(valid_votes_i * (p_i - q_i)^2) / sum(valid_votes_i)` for `i ∈ C`, with numerator, positive denominator `sum(valid_votes_i)`, fixed-point precision and one final rounding rule bound to policy. Label votes never stand in for detector predictions. Preserve R100's independent-outcome hard-negative join. `Method::UnifiedQuery` supplies retrieved evidence and `Method::DecisionEval` seals the receipt. The scorer and AU answer/topic/label producers must bind their independent provenance. WebUI remains a consumer. Source objective EG:18 SHA-256 `d978b4b4201059c2e7bcc7a78def0d5845cf7db1e361f2f50ee57f8bcc5733ae`. | Serve fixtures with known expected/covered topic sets and non-unanimous labels. Independently recompute each scope's summed topic numerator and denominator. Recompute weighted Brier numerator and positive vote denominator from distinct predictions and votes. Hold labels fixed and vary only `p_i`. The Brier score must change by the formula while topic coverage remains constant. Refuse empty query, topic or claim sets. Refuse missing/extra predictions or labels, duplicate annotator refs, zero vote denominator or probabilities outside [0,1]. Also refuse label access by the scorer and stale answer/corpus/evidence/scorer/evaluator/policy revisions or digests. Read back exact prediction, label, topic-set and score bindings. AU/scorer producer receipts remain separately required. No implementation is claimed. |

| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R001.100` | **Quantitative physical/safety bounds before recommendation surfacing.** Extend `Method::Decide` with `RecommendationSafetyV1`. Bind recommendation id/revision/digest, candidate result refs and policy id/revision/digest. Each `SafetyBoundV1` binds its id/revision/digest, metric id/unit, comparator, limit and measured value/source revision. Return each checked bound and a verdict (`pass` or `withheld`). Include the exact violated bound id, configured limit and measured value on refusal. Missing, stale, non-finite, unit-incompatible or unmeasured bounds, unsupported comparators and any out-of-bound metric withhold the recommendation. EG emits the bound evaluation result only. GraphOS must invoke and validate it before surfacing a recommendation. Its exact producer/consumer operation and served receipt are a pending companion prerequisite, not covered by this EG definition. Preserve partial `AU-HARNESS-R021.4/.6/.7`. Those do not prove a pre-surface gate. Source objective GRAPHOS:19 SHA-256 `cd33b64c1d759f3c7dd0a83112b77955d25a49c69d20288fe4c917716c711ff6`. | Served cases place an in-bound and out-of-bound recommendation through `Decide`. Verify exact units, values, limits and the withheld bound id. Refuse absent/stale bounds, NaN/infinity, unit mismatch, unsupported comparator, missing measurement and stale recommendation/policy digests. Assert GraphOS cannot surface until it supplies the separately served pre-surface validation receipt. That GraphOS operation remains pending and this requirement does not claim it exists. |

| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R135.11` | **Behavior.** The engine must consolidate submitted insights and rank them by context match and success. It must select insights for a task context. Ties must order by insight id. | A fixture of insights yields the expected consolidation and selection order. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R135.12` | **Wiring.** The Harness observes outcomes and submits them to the engine. The engine owns storage, consolidation and selection. | `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R135.13` | **Refusal.** The engine must refuse a submission that has no source run. It must refuse a selection request with an unknown context signature. Each refusal must carry a typed reason. | Each refusal case returns its typed reason and stores nothing. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R135.14` | **Evidence.** The engine must emit premises for the selected insights, their source runs and the selection revision. The Harness must record which insights it injected. | A trace links each injected insight to its source runs. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R135.15` | **Hot-path proof.** The entry point is the served Decide wire method. The integration test is tests/decide/test_insight_selection_served.py::test_post_run_write_then_context_injection. It writes after a run, selects later, and checks injection with source-run provenance. A source-less submission must refuse. | That test passes against the served engine. |

## Reviewed mapped definitions — 2026-10-12

These append-only children preserve the existing parent contracts named below. All remain SPECIFIED. This definition records planned acceptance and does not claim implementation or served receipts.

| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R042.100` | **Tool-path outcome history and repeated-failure skip rule.** Model `ToolPathHistory` by tenant, connector, capability and stable path id/version. Bind observation window, sample/success/failure counts, as-of time and source invocation ids. Behavior ranks only already-admitted equivalent paths by observed success rate. Skip a path only when the versioned decision policy declares a minimum sample and failure threshold and both are met. Missing, stale or undersampled history is `unknown` and cannot suppress a path. Wiring adds the evidence to the existing R042 Decide advisory and consumes current attempt outcomes from SDK `ordered_equivalent_fallback`. It must not replace the SDK declared fallback order or execute writes. Evidence binds policy, candidate paths and observation window. Producer/consumer boundary. EG R042 remains evaluate-only connector tool-choice advisory; SDK owns ordered transport fallback and current attempt outcomes. Preserve existing parent obligations: `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R042; SDK-SOURCE-INGEST-R012.2; preserve deterministic admitted fallback`. Source record `AU:28` SHA-256 `9dac8e5f41f6948885215836733ce3116b8246ca5c9c4725a12a62bdb11cb400`. Parent `R042` remains unchanged. | Planned served acceptance. In served Decide, prefer adequately sampled higher-success paths. Skip threshold-qualified repeated failures. Preserve declared order for missing, stale or undersampled history. Verify the advisory cannot trigger write-back and every score recomputes from its bound counts. Owner-source review only. No runtime/test receipt is inferred. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R042.101` | **Per-model measured tool necessity and every-call mismatch trace.** Define `ToolNecessityMeasurement`. Bind model id/revision, tool/capability id and task-cohort digest. Record paired enabled/disabled trial identities, outcome metric, sample counts, threshold, measurement time and digest. A measurement is `necessary`, `not_necessary` or `unknown` only under the declared minimum-sample and outcome-delta policy. No data, stale model revision or insufficient pairs yields `unknown`. Compare every AU tool-call decision to the current model/tool measurement. Emit a mismatch trace event with run, step, decision, model revision, measurement digest and mismatch kind (`unnecessary_call` or `omitted_necessary_tool`). The EG R042 decision remains authoritative; AU consumes at the existing per-tool span path. Producer/consumer boundary. R134.2 relevance is only a candidate prefilter. AU-HARNESS-R018.5 generic profile metrics do not measure tool necessity. AU run_agent per-tool spans are the consumer boundary. Preserve existing parent obligations: `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R134.2; AU-HARNESS-R018.5; AU-HARNESS-R024.2/.4`. For each paired fixture, show necessary/not-necessary/unknown results. At the AU call boundary prove every disagreement creates a linked trace event and matching decisions create none. Stale or insufficient evidence must not become a necessity verdict. Served EG and AU receipts remain separate and linked by decision/measurement ids. Owner-source review only. No runtime/test receipt is inferred. |

| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R151.4` | **Refusal.** Invalid importance (not in [0,1]), missing evidence or zero feature count refuse. Proving test: `src/server/handlers/decide/embedding_tests.rs::invalid_importance_refuses`. | Negative cases cover out-of-range weights, missing evidence. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R151.5` | **Evidence.** Bind each feature id, importance weight and evidence digest. Record penalty objective and applied loss/importance scales. Proving test: `src/server/handlers/decide/embedding_tests.rs::penalty_evidence`. | Record lists all feature weights, evidence and penalty scale. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R151.6` | **Hot-path proof.** Serve embedding with importance weights. Apply weighted penalty. Verify post-sync consolidation is queued, not routed. Proving test: `tests/decide/test_importance_penalty_served.py::test_penalty_and_consolidation_queue`. | Penalty computed; consolidation separately queued post-sync. |

| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R100.100` | **Pinned retrieval evaluation and authorized context spans.** Extend `DecisionEval` with `RetrievalRankingSubmit` and typed `RetrievalRankingV1` request/receipt. Serve it through `Method::DecisionEval` and `src/server/handlers/decide/jobs.rs`/`stat_jobs.rs`. Bind corpus and binary relevance-label set ids/revisions/digests. Bind a query-set id/revision/digest with at least one query and `k` (1..1000). Record evaluator revision and at least two registered algorithm ids/versions/settings digests. For each algorithm, define `hits_at_k = numerator / denominator`. The numerator counts labeled queries with a relevant top-k item. The denominator is the complete identically labeled query set. Return both integers, the exact query/result digest and metric value. Compare algorithms only on identical input digests. Return typed refusal for empty query sets, zero denominator or missing labels. Also refuse corpus drift, incomplete result coverage and mismatched settings; never fabricate a metric. Add public `Method::RetrievalContextSearch` (`RetrievalContextSearchV1`) on the authenticated query/read path. Return document id/revision/digest and half-open UTF-8 byte offsets into original stored text for every matched n-gram/context span. Search may normalize into a separate NFC index only when it returns that index digest and a validated normalized-to-original byte map. The public offsets always address the stored-text digest, never the normalized buffer. Also return query/index/authorization revisions and an opaque snapshot-bound cursor. Context reads are tenant-authorized, capped at 100 spans and 8 KiB per span. Denied context returns no protected count or offset. Existing `Method::UnifiedQuery`/retrieval paths supply algorithm results; `DecisionEval` seals the evaluation receipt and R100 continues joining cited evidence to independent outcomes/hard negatives. The WebUI comparison panel and labels remain consumers, not truth producers. Consumer: `WEBUI-APPS-R001.1`. WEBUI:6 source objective: “An operator inspects and compares retrieval quality interactively: hits-at-k metrics, n-gram highlighting, side-by-side algorithm comparison and context search.” SHA-256 `3dc3c464a10a5178a413463f2b43baeb015732f5b8df508f6dbab9c37cc1165d`. | Use a fixed served corpus and independently pinned labels. Run two registered retrieval algorithms over the same query set and k. Verify exact hits-at-k numerators/denominators, input/result digests and receipt readback. Search composed and decomposed Unicode plus multibyte text. Verify every returned half-open offset slices the original stored UTF-8 bytes to the exact match and binds that stored-text digest. If NFC indexing is used, verify its separate digest and normalized-to-original byte map. Refuse empty query sets/zero denominator and invalid normalization maps. Also verify bounded contexts. Refuse missing labels, incompatible revisions/settings, incomplete algorithm results, stale cursor and denied context without leaking counts. The WebUI side-by-side consumer must use the exact same evaluation receipt. Local RRF is not relevance truth. No runtime or owner receipt is claimed. |
