| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R172` | **Capability designation index moved from agent-utilities (rollup).** EG owns capability designation. Given a task embedding and optional required capabilities, the engine selects the agents, tools and skills that handle the task. The engine filters by capability, tenant and policy. It ranks by cosine similarity, a learned outcome reward and an ontology prior. It explains why each candidate is eligible. This replaces the deleted AU module `agent_utilities/knowledge_graph/retrieval/capability_index.py` (`CapabilityIndex`, `Designation`, `compute_eligibility`) and its feeders. The EG implementation is native Rust only. No designation, scoring or reward logic goes into `epistemic_graph/` beyond the generated client binding. Children `.1` to `.11` deliver it. Child `.13` applies scoped adapters and drift refresh to the index. Producer: the engine-resident index. Consumer: the AU run loop through a typed designation port that receives EG answers. | See each child. R172 is LANDED when every child is VERIFIED. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R172.1` | **Model.** Add `crates/eg-types/src/decision/capability_designation.rs`. Define `CapabilityRecordV1{tenant_id: Option<TenantId>, entity_id, entity_kind (closed: agent, tool, skill), capabilities: BTreeSet<CapabilityIri>, ontology_type: Option<ClassIri>, policy_tags: BTreeSet<String>, swappable_with: BTreeSet<EntityId>, embedding_version, dim: u16, record_revision: u64}`. A record with no tenant is a commons record. Define `CapabilityRewardV1{tenant_id, entity_id, reward_q32, outcome_count: u64, generation: u32, updated_revision}`; the neutral value is 0.5. Define `CapabilityDesignationQuestionV1{tenant_id, query_vector{embedding_version, dim, f32le_b64}, required_capabilities, required_policy_tags, k: u8, weights{reward_q32, prior_q32}, ontology_prior: Option<Vec<(EntityId, q32)>>}`. Define `CapabilityDesignationV1{designations: Vec<DesignatedCapabilityV1>, candidate_pool_size, capability_filtered, index_epoch, embedding_version}`. Each `DesignatedCapabilityV1` binds `entity_id, score_q32, cosine_q32, reward_q32, prior_q32, ontology_type, capabilities, alternatives, eligibility: CapabilityEligibilityV1`. `CapabilityEligibilityV1` keeps the field set of AU `compute_eligibility`. The capability fields are capabilities, required_caps, missing_caps and capabilities_matched. The tenant fields are tenant, required_tenant and a tri-state tenant_match. The policy fields are policy_tags, required_policy_tags, missing_policy_tags and policy_matched. The other fields are reward, ontology_type, eligible and subsumption_paths. Scores and weights are Q32 integers on the wire. Only the query vector is f32, as little-endian base64. Proving test: `crates/eg-types/src/decision/capability_designation.rs::tests::designation_types_round_trip`. | A serde round trip keeps every field. It rejects an unknown entity kind, a dim of zero, and a base64 vector whose length is not `4 * dim`. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R172.2` | **Behavior.** Add the resident index in new module `crates/eg-compute/src/capability_index/`. It reuses `eg-ann` (`flat` and `hnsw`) and stores nothing itself. Keep one index per tenant shard plus one commons shard. Intern entity ids and capability IRIs to dense `u32` ids. Store capability postings as compressed bitmaps (`capability -> bitmap of entity ids`). Store policy-tag postings the same way. Store vectors in a columnar SQ8 arena (one byte per dimension) with an f32 re-score tier for the final top-k. Metadata is a struct-of-arrays indexed by the dense id. Apply each record change as an incremental upsert or tombstone. Never rebuild the index for one change. Compact tombstones when they exceed 20 percent (named default `tombstone_compact_ratio`). Build is O(N log N) for the ANN graph. Build is O(N c) for postings, with c capabilities per record. A capability filter costs one bitmap intersection per required capability, plus a union over subsumed subtypes. If the filtered pool is at most `flat_threshold` (default 4096), rank by an exact SQ8 scan with f32 re-score. Otherwise, run filtered HNSW traversal with the bitmap as the admit predicate. If `eg-ann` hnsw search has no predicate, add one. Readers use a lock-free snapshot (an atomically swapped `Arc` per shard). One writer per shard publishes a new epoch after each change batch. The query path takes no global lock. Memory is bounded by `max_records_per_shard` (default 1,000,000); an upsert past the bound refuses. It does not evict silently. This replaces AU `CapabilityIndex.add`, `remove`, `build_from_edges`, `_candidate_ids` and the 4096-entry LRU cache. Proving test: `crates/eg-compute/src/capability_index/tests.rs::incremental_upsert_and_tombstone_match_rebuild`. | Build an index by N incremental upserts and deletes. It returns the same top-k as a full rebuild of the surviving records. A change touches only the changed id. A bound overflow refuses with no partial write. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R172.3` | **Behavior.** Add `compute_capability_eligibility(record, question, projection)` in `capability_designation.rs` as a pure function. Each rule is a named replaceable default. `capability_rule`: the record meets a required capability T when it declares T. It also meets T when it declares a subtype of T. The composed GraphSchema `rdfs:subClassOf` closure defines subtypes. The first matching declared class in sorted order gives the reported subsumption path. `tenant_rule`: a commons record is visible to every tenant; a record of a different tenant is never a candidate. Tenant filtering runs before any score or count (R013). `policy_rule`: fail-closed; a record must carry every required policy tag, and an untagged record fails any non-empty requirement. `eligible` is the AND of the gates that the question asked for. An omitted filter never disqualifies. The subsumption projection comes from the engine's own `OwlReason` closure at the question's schema digest. It is not supplied by the caller. This replaces AU `compute_eligibility`, `_satisfied_required_caps`, `_tenant_match`, `_is_eligible` and `CapabilityIndex.explain`. AU acceptance cases: `tests/retrieval/test_capability_index.py::test_injected_hierarchy_matches_declared_subtype`, `::test_policy_filter_excludes_ineligible_candidate`, `::test_tenant_filter_scopes_candidates_but_admits_unscoped`, `::test_combined_capability_tenant_policy_filters`, `::test_explain_reports_eligible_candidate_features`, `::test_explain_reports_why_an_ineligible_candidate_was_excluded`. Proving test: `crates/eg-types/src/decision/capability_designation.rs::tests::eligibility_gates_match_au_cases`. | Each listed AU case, re-expressed as a fixture, gives the same eligible verdict, missing sets and subsumption path. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R172.4` | **Behavior.** Add `rank_capability_candidates(pool, question, rewards, policy)` in `crates/eg-compute/src/capability_index/rank.rs`. L2-normalize the stored and query vectors; similarity is the inner product. Each rule is a named replaceable default. `oversample_rule`: take `min(pool_size, max(k, 4k))` candidates by pure cosine. `reward_blend`: add `reward_weight * (reward - 0.5)`; default `reward_weight` 0.15. `prior_blend`: add `prior_weight * (prior - 0.5)`; default `prior_weight` 0.15. `default_ontology_prior` applies when the question gives no prior and records carry an ontology type. The modal type of the oversampled pool gets 1.0. Other types get 0.5. With no stored types, the prior is off. A weight of zero turns its term off. With both terms off the order is pure cosine. Ties order by entity id, ascending. Return at most k designations, each with its swappable alternatives sorted by id. This replaces AU `CapabilityIndex.designate`, `_blend_ranked`, `_resolve_ontology_prior`, `_rank_brute_force`, `_rank_via_hnsw` and `alternatives`. AU acceptance cases: `tests/retrieval/test_capability_index.py::test_ranking_returns_planted_top1`, `::test_capability_filter_shrinks_results`, `::test_alternatives_returns_swappable_partner`, and all five cases in `tests/retrieval/test_kg_2_44b_ontology_prior.py`. Proving test: `crates/eg-compute/src/capability_index/tests.rs::blend_order_matches_au_cases`. | Fixtures show planted top-1 recovery and type-coherent top-k. They show exact cosine parity with both weights at zero. They show an explicit prior that overrides the default, and id-ordered ties. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R172.5` | **Behavior.** Add `apply_capability_outcome(reward, outcome, policy)` in `capability_designation.rs`. The outcome is `success: bool` (1 or 0) or an explicit reward clamped to [0, 1]. The update is an exponential moving average: `new = (1 - alpha) * old + alpha * observed`, with named default `alpha` 0.3 and neutral start 0.5. All arithmetic is Q32 fixed point, so replay is deterministic (R007). `generation_erasure` applies when a writer upserts a record again with a changed vector. If the cosine distance to the stored vector is above 0.25, reset the reward to neutral. Then increment `generation`. `selective_erase(ids)` resets only the named ids and is order-independent. `decay(factor)` moves every reward toward 0.5 by `0.5 + (r - 0.5) * factor`, with default factor 0.99. A durable reward seeds a record only when the shard has no newer reward for it. Rewards persist in a new Agent Library table `capability_reward` through `AgentLibraryStore` in redb. They survive restart. This replaces AU `record_outcome`, `reward_of`, `_maybe_erase_stale_reward`, `selective_erase_rewards`, `decay_rewards`, and the AU durable EMA in `retrieval/durable_outcome_store.py`. AU acceptance cases: all five cases in `tests/retrieval/test_reward_writeback.py`, `tests/retrieval/test_capability_index.py::test_material_reembed_erases_stale_reward`, `::test_selective_erase_rewards_is_targeted_and_order_independent`, and `tests/unit/graph/test_capability_routing_x4.py::test_bandit_prefers_the_historically_better_tool_over_a_marginally_closer_one`. Proving test: `crates/eg-types/src/decision/capability_designation.rs::tests::reward_ema_erasure_and_decay`. | Fixtures show a bounded EMA. A material re-embed resets reward, and a minor re-embed keeps it. Fixtures also show targeted erasure, decay toward neutral, and a reward that survives store reopen. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R172.6` | **Behavior.** Each shard pins one `embedding_version` and one `dim` at its first record. A record or question with a different version or dim is refused. Re-indexing to a new version builds a second shard generation beside the live one and swaps it atomically. Readers never see a mixed generation. A drained shard accepts a new version. A generation swap is atomic to readers. |

| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R172.10` | **Behavior.** The index meets measured budgets. Reference load: N = 100,000 records, dim = 1024, 8 capabilities per record, k = 5, on one build-host core. Filtered case: one required capability selects at most 5 percent of records. Its budget is 2 ms at p50 and 10 ms at p99 or less. Unfiltered case: the budget is 3 ms at p50 and 15 ms at p99 or less. Its recall@5 is at least 0.95 against an exact scan. Incremental upsert: the budget is 1 ms at p99 or less, without durable commit. Resident memory is at most `1.25 * N * dim` bytes for vectors plus 96 bytes per record for metadata and postings. At the reference load this is about 132 MiB. Query cost is sub-linear in N for the unfiltered and large-pool paths. Read throughput scales with reader threads, because no global lock is on the query path. Benchmark: `benches/capability_designation_bench.rs`. | The benchmark reports p50, p99, recall@5 and resident bytes at the reference load and at N = 10,000. Each value is within its budget. |

Add closed `AdapterSide` values `query` and `index`. Add closed `OutcomeSource` values `retrieval_outcome`, `run_outcome` and `capability_outcome`. Extend `AdapterFitRequest` with `scope`, `side` and `sources`. Extend `QueryAdapterBody` with `revision: u32` and `parent_digest: Option<String>`. Add `PointerRef::ScopedAdapter{graph, scope}` at `crates/eg-types/src/decision/statistical/retrieval.rs:263`. | `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R174.7.2` | **Behavior.** Extend the fit in `crates/eg-numeric/src/decision/adapter.rs`. The transform is `W = I + sum(g_i * u_i * u_i^T)` with rank 1 to 8 and orthonormal units. Rank 1 to 8 covers the low-rank layer; a diagonal projection is rank `d` and is out of scope. Cited rows are positives. Retrieved rows that are not cited are hard negatives (`EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R100`, `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R100.102`). Capability outcomes (`EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R172.5`) weight task-to-entity pairs by reward. All arithmetic is Q30 fixed point with a seeded order, so a refit replays bit for bit. The held-out split hashes each decision id. Keep needs the existing Wilson lower bound above one half and adapted MRR at least base MRR. After activation, a monitor dual-scores new outcomes against the base. It rolls back when the Wilson upper bound falls below one half over at least 50 outcomes. Resolution order is capability, then corpus, then graph, then base. An `index` adapter transforms stored vectors at read, with no embedding endpoint call. Budgets: fit at most 30 s for 100,000 examples at `d = 1024` and rank 8 on one core. Fit memory is at most 64 MiB. A resident adapter is at most 40 KiB. A tenant holds at most 64 active adapters. | Unit tests cover deterministic refit, the keep and revert rules, scope resolution order and the memory cap. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R174.7.3` | **Wiring.** The fit enters `DecisionLogOp::Learn` at `crates/eg-types/src/decision/statistical/log.rs:398` through `LearningWrite::FitAdapter` and `stat_adapter.rs`. Serving resolves the scoped adapter in `src/server/handlers/decide/served_adapter.rs` and applies it at `src/server/handlers/graph_ops/semantic.rs:233` on `Discover` and `SemanticSearch`. The capability index applies it before `rank_capability_candidates` (`EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R172.13`). An `index` adapter materializes through the generation cycle of `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R174` with no endpoint call. Fits run as `eg-jobs` analytics jobs on a background lease from `AcquireCapacity`. Non-urgent runs take downtime priority from the downtime scheduling of `EG-DURABLE-KERNEL-R101`. | A dispatch test routes a scoped fit, an activation and a served query through each named call site. |

Fewer held-out outcomes than `min_eval_items` must refuse with `insufficient_outcomes`. A space digest that differs from the graph's space must refuse with `adapter_space_mismatch`. Staleness reuses the `stale_index` test of `EG-FEDERATED-QUERY-R089.2`. Named defaults: shift 0.2, vocabulary growth 5 points, quality decay 10 percent. | `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R750.750` | **Model: Bounded multi-step reasoning search with explicit budgets.** A reasoning strategy is a closed enum over chain, tree, graph and planning-with-world-model search. Each strategy runs over an arena of thought nodes. The decision ladder gives it a deterministic budget for nodes, depth, branching and tokens. A stored `ReasoningPolicy` selects the strategy per task class. Types: `ReasoningStrategy` (Chain, Tree, Graph, Planning), `ReasoningState`, `BudgetLedger`, `ThoughtNode` in a bump arena indexed by `u32`. Concurrency: read paths are lock-free over immutable snapshots; writes go through the single write authority. | Unit tests assert the stated behaviour and golden vectors replay identically on every supported target. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R750.751` | **Behavior: Bounded multi-step reasoning search with explicit budgets.** Expansion, scoring, pruning, aggregation and merge are pure functions of the state and the fixed-point scores. The same inputs replay to the same best path and digest. An exhausted budget returns a typed best-so-far result with a certified-gap flag. It never continues without a bound. | Unit tests assert the stated behaviour and golden vectors replay identically on every supported target. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R750.752` | **Refusal: Bounded multi-step reasoning search with explicit budgets.** The engine refuses four inputs with typed errors. The fourth is a score that is not a fixed-point value. | Unit tests assert the stated behaviour and golden vectors replay identically on every supported target. |

Concurrency: read paths are lock-free over immutable snapshots; writes go through the single write authority. | Unit tests assert the stated behaviour and golden vectors replay identically on every supported target. |

Types: `DriftReading`, `Wasserstein1`, `PopulationSpread`, `TrustLedger`, `IrreversibilityClass`, `CorrigibilityVerdict`. | `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R650` | **Capability descriptors and request-scoped designation (rollup).** EG holds one versioned descriptor per capability. A descriptor carries typed inputs and outputs, side effects, data types, scopes, cost, latency, reliability, locality and approval class. EG projects the classified closure for a request. The harness receives the designation; it holds no descriptor index. The Rust home is `crates/eg-types/src/agent_component/capability_descriptor.rs`. Satisfied only when children `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R650.1`-`EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R650.6` are satisfied. | See each child. |

Types are serde-stable, tenant-scoped, and included in the contract digest. | A contract test lists the types in `contract/methods.json` and fails on an undeclared field change. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R650.2` | **Behavior.** Persist a descriptor as properties of the capability node. Derive reliability from the learned reward and do not store it. Generate the power descriptor from the registry and the capability ledger. Project the request-scoped closure from OWL classification in O(closure size). | Unit tests assert the behavior on fixed fixtures and a property test asserts determinism across runs and thread counts. |

| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R700` | **Belief revision recomputes confidence and explains every change (rollup).** The engine recomputes the confidence of a claim from its supporting and opposing evidence. It records an explanation of each revision, detects opposing claims, and revalidates stale truth-maintenance materializations. Every result is a proposal. No claim is retracted without a separate explicit authorization (see R034). Implemented in eg-reason, eg-core. | Every child `.1` to `.6` is verified; the rollup is landed only when all are. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R700.1` | **Model.** `BeliefRevision { claim, prior, posterior, support: Vec<EvidenceRef>, oppose: Vec<EvidenceRef>, reason }` and `FrictionFinding { left, right, similarity, opposes }` in `crates/eg-types`, keyed by claim id in a `BTreeMap` so a pass is deterministic. | Compile-time test: the types reject the invalid shapes named in `.4`; golden serialization vector round-trips. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R700.2` | **Behavior.** A revision pass walks claims whose evidence changed since the last pass. Its time is linear in changed claims plus their evidence edges. Opposition candidates come from an inverted token index (sub-linear per claim). A typed negation and antonym check confirms them. The pass revalidates stale materializations against the current assertion generation. It re-derives them only when their premises changed. | Unit tests cover the stated complexity bound on fixtures of 10, 1,000 and 100,000 items. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R700.3` | **Wiring.** Handler `BeliefRevise` in `src/server/handlers`, wired to the outbox so only claims touched by a committed batch enter a pass. Per-graph passes run on the maintenance lane; passes on different graphs run in parallel, passes on one graph are serialized. | The served test in `.6` reaches the behavior through the wire method, not through a unit entry point. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R700.4` | **Refusal.** A revision that lowers a claim below the retraction threshold returns a `RetractionProposal` with evidence class `claim`. It never mutates the claim. A revision with a missing premise refuses with a typed reason. The reason names the premise. | Negative tests assert each refusal code and that durable state is unchanged. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R700.5` | **Evidence.** Each `BeliefRevision` stores its explanation and the evidence class of its weakest premise. The explanation reproduces the posterior from the recorded inputs. | A read of the stored record reproduces the recorded decision from its recorded inputs. |

| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R802` | **Web fetch selection over search candidates (rollup).** The engine must choose the web candidates to fetch. It ranks candidates and removes duplicates by canonical URL and content digest. It skips a held Document that is still fresh. It reads only the RLS-visible Documents of the request graph and enforces a fetch budget. No model runs. Children `.1` to `.6` deliver it. | See each child. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R802.1` | **Model.** `crates/eg-types/src/decision/web_fetch_selection.rs` holds `SelectWebPagesRequest{query, candidates, max_fetches, freshness_window_s, now_ms}`, `WebCandidate{url, title, snippet, engine, rank, content_digest, published_at_ms}` and `SelectWebPagesAnswer{query_digest, selected, skipped, max_fetches, considered, now_ms}`. `max_fetches` ranges from 1 to 32. Closed skip reasons are `duplicate_url`, `duplicate_digest`, `held_fresh`, `invalid_url` and `over_budget`. Proving test: `web_fetch_selection.rs::tests::question_round_trips`. | A serde round trip keeps every field and rejects 33 fetches or an unknown skip reason. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R802.2` | **Behavior.** `crates/eg-numeric/src/decision/web_fetch_selection.rs` canonicalizes each URL. Rows with one canonical URL fuse by reciprocal rank fusion with k = 60. A digest that the graph holds, or that another row carries, is skipped as `duplicate_digest`. A held Document younger than `freshness_window_s` is skipped as `held_fresh`. The default window is 86,400 s. A stale held Document is selectable as a refresh. The score is `0.5 relevance + 0.3 rank + 0.2 freshness` in integer millionths. The budget keeps the top `max_fetches`. Cost is O(c log c) in candidates. Proving test: `crates/eg-numeric/src/decision/web_fetch_selection.rs::tests::ranks_dedupes_and_skips_fresh`. | Unit tests cover each skip reason, the ranking order and the budget. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R802.3` | **Wiring.** The served `SelectWebPages` method reaches `src/server/dispatch/graph_pipeline/web_ingest.rs` after graph ACL. It reads the RLS-visible `Document` rows of the request graph as held pages. The production caller is the arbora operation `ARBORA-OPS-R067`. Proving test: `tests/web_document_enrich_served.rs::test_web_page_enriches_once_for_both_backends`. | A served `SelectWebPages` call skips the held and duplicate pages. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R802.4` | **Refusal.** An empty query must refuse with `WEB_SELECTION_QUERY_MISSING`. An empty candidate set must refuse with `WEB_SELECTION_NO_CANDIDATES`. More than 256 candidates must refuse with `WEB_SELECTION_TOO_MANY_CANDIDATES`. A budget outside 1 to 32 must refuse with `WEB_SELECTION_BUDGET_INVALID`. Proving test: `crates/eg-numeric/src/decision/web_fetch_selection.rs::tests::web_fetch_refusals`. | Each case returns its code and selects no candidate. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R802.5` | **Evidence.** The answer must hold one entry per candidate, selected or skipped. A selected entry names the canonical URL, the engines, the score, its three components and its reasons. A skipped entry names its reason and a detail. Proving test: `crates/eg-numeric/src/decision/web_fetch_selection.rs::tests::premises_per_candidate`. | Each candidate has one entry, and the selected order follows the score. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R802.6` | **Hot-path proof.** The entry point is the served `SelectWebPages` method. The benchmark selects from 50 candidates, ten of them held and fresh. The answer must hold `max_fetches` or fewer, no held fresh URL and no duplicate. The answer must stay under 3 ms p99 for 50 candidates. The benchmark is `crates/eg-numeric/benches/web_fetch_selection.rs`. Proving test: `tests/web_document_enrich_served.rs::test_web_page_enriches_once_for_both_backends`. | The served test passes, and the benchmark meets the budget. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R803` | **Contrastive harness composition decision (rollup).** The engine composes the adopted harness for one task from the probed candidates. A caller presents two to eight candidate component versions, the identical probe conditions and the measured contrasts. The engine adopts a candidate only when its measured delta shows improvement. It abstains and keeps the current harness when no candidate improves. Children `.1` through `.6` cover model, behavior, wiring, refusal, evidence and hot-path proof. [arXiv:2610.14224] | See each child. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R803.1` | **Model.** Add `HarnessContrastDecision{task_ref, task_class, candidate_version_refs, conditions_digest, probe_trace_ids, deltas, adopted_version_ref, abstain_reason}` in `crates/eg-types/src/decision/harness_contrast.rs`. `deltas` holds one `HarnessContrast` of EG-ORCH-VOCAB-R802.1 per candidate. `abstain_reason` is the closed set `no_improvement`, `trace_mismatch` or `insufficient_evidence`. Planned test: `crates/eg-types/src/decision/harness_contrast.rs::tests::decision_round_trips`. | The serde round trip keeps every field and rejects an open abstain reason. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R803.2` | **Behavior.** The caller drives the probes under an identical task, seed, model and budget; the candidate count k runs from 2 to 8. The engine reads the probe traces and the contrasts. It adopts a candidate when its `eval_score_delta` is positive and no other candidate beats it, with cost as the tie-break. It abstains with `no_improvement` when every delta is zero or negative. An abstention keeps the current harness and writes only the decision record. Planned test: `crates/eg-types/src/decision/harness_contrast.rs::tests::adoption_requires_positive_delta`. | Unit tests cover adoption, tie-break and abstention. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R803.3` | **Wiring.** The served `Decide` method accepts the `HARNESS_CONTRAST` question kind through `DecideText`. The answer stores as a `DecisionRecord` through `DecisionCommit` (EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R019, EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R047). The production caller is the harness cycle of AU-HARNESS-CYCLE-R001.5; it adopts the result through the arbora operation ARBORA-OPS-R068.4. Planned test: `tests/decide/test_harness_contrast_served.py::test_contrast_decide_stores_record`. | A served `Decide` call stores one decision record. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R803.4` | **Refusal.** Four inputs refuse with a typed code and write nothing. The first is a candidate set under two, with `HARNESS_CONTRAST_MIN_CANDIDATES`. The second is a probe trace whose conditions digest differs from the request, with `HARNESS_CONTRAST_TRACE_MISMATCH`. The third is a candidate version reference the graph does not hold, with `HARNESS_COMPONENT_VERSION_UNKNOWN`. The fourth is a candidate count over 8, with `HARNESS_CONTRAST_TOO_MANY_CANDIDATES`. Planned test: `crates/eg-types/src/decision/harness_contrast.rs::tests::contrast_refusals`. | Each case returns its code and stores no record. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R803.5` | **Evidence.** The record pins the request digest, each candidate version digest, the probe trace ids and the per-candidate deltas. A rerun with the same inputs produces the same record digest. The adopted record cites the superseded adoption of the same agent and task class through EG-ORCH-VOCAB-R802.4. Planned test: `crates/eg-types/src/decision/harness_contrast.rs::tests::record_pins_evidence_and_replays`. | The rerun digest matches, and the supersession citation resolves. |
| `EG-DECISION-ENGINE-R803.6` | **Hot-path proof.** The entry point is the served `Decide` call. The test presents two candidates, one improving and one neutral, and checks the adoption and the abstention. The benchmark decides over 8 candidates with 200 stored contrasts. It stays under 5 ms p99. The benchmark is `crates/eg-numeric/benches/harness_contrast.rs`. Planned test: `tests/decide/test_harness_contrast_served.py::test_contrast_hot_path_served`. | The served test passes, and the benchmark meets the budget. |
